ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u7	Thank you very much. We proceed with the examination of item 1 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Act on collective supply of water and collective discharge of waste water and certain other laws (Print No 1905). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Environment of Mr Mariusz Gajda to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u8	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Today I have the honour to present to you a government bill amending the Act on collective water supply and collective waste water discharge and some other laws. Although at the moment this law is subject to the Minister of Infrastructure and Construction under the Law on Departments, according to the provisions of the Water Law of 20 July 2018 from 1 January 2018, the Minister responsible for water management will supervise collective water supply and waste water discharge. The amendment of this law is a consequence of two things: firstly, the consequence of the Water Law Act and secondly, the implementation of the recommendations of the Supreme Audit Chamber of 2017 concerning price formation for water supply and waste water discharge. The current arrangements for supervising and regulating the performance of collective water supply and waste water disposal tasks do not allow sufficient effective supervision of the performance of the tasks by water and sewage service providers. It is currently the case that the tariffs for water supply and waste water discharge, i.e. those prices paid by every citizen using these services, are approved by a resolution of the municipal council. At the same time, most water and sewage companies are majority or rather 100% owned by the same municipality. There is therefore a conflict of interest, namely that the person who sets the tariff himself approves the tariff. The situation is that, formally, the water and sewage company prepares the draft tariff and then the municipality board approves the tariff. This law introduces a fundamental amendment, namely the approval of tariffs will be carried out by the regulator of water and waste water prices. This regulator of water and sewage prices will be the director of the Regional Water Management Board of Polish Waters. Ladies and gentlemen! This law, in addition to the approval of tariffs, regulates two other matters in general. Firstly, the same regulator will give an opinion on the regulations for the supply of water and waste water. It is an extremely important document which prepares the municipality and which talks about the rights and obligations of both consumers and suppliers when it comes to water and sewage. It should be stressed that, in fact, water and sewage companies are monopolisists in their territory. There is therefore a need to increase surveillance. But it will only be an opinion, because these are the tasks of the municipalities themselves. It'll only be an opinion about the rules. The next regulation will be the approval of tariffs. Although this is a community's own task, a similar situation exists on the market for the supply of heat, which is also the task of the municipality itself, and the tariffs for heat are approved by the Energy Regulatory Office. So it's kind of an estimate of what's in the tariff range for heat. Now I'm going to be more specific about what these problems are. The regulation of the bill will aim at legal protection of the interests of the recipients of services by establishing an independent and specialised regulatory body which will carry out additional supervision over the implementation of collective water supply and waste water disposal tasks. The exercise of regulatory powers in relation to collective water supply and waste water disposal tasks will consist in particular of an opinion by the regulatory authority on the draft rules for the supply of water and waste water discharge and the approval of tariffs for collective water supply and collective waste water discharge. The regulatory authority's competence will also include the settlement of disputes between water and sewage undertakings and recipients of services and the imposition of fines in the event of failure to comply with the obligation to approve tariffs by the regulatory authority or to apply approved tariffs, in excess of the prices or rates of charges. The current market regulation model for the provision of water and sewage services does not provide objective or independent supervision in the field of collective water supply and waste water discharge. In accordance with the provisions of the Act on collective water supply, the regulation of the market was entrusted to the municipality and is based on the division of competences between the mayor, the mayor, the president and the council of the municipality. Regulation cited above. the laws assign to local government units a number of tasks to which tariff approval in particular is required. In the current state of law, the municipality approves the tariff for collective water supply and the collective discharge of waste water on the basis of the application submitted by the water and sewerage undertaking for the approval of the tariff, while it should be stressed that the services relating to collective water supply and the collective discharge of waste water are mainly provided by budgetary establishments or water and sewage undertakings operating in the form of commercial law companies in which the municipality is a majority or sole owner. Consequently, this situation could lead to unjustified increases in prices and charges for collective water supply and collective sewage disposal, as demonstrated inter alia. reports from the Supreme Audit Chamber. In addition, local government units, despite the available tools, do not in particular control the right to verify tariff applications. They often do not exercise their powers and thus do not sufficiently protect service users from systematic price increases. The average price of water and sewage disposal in 2008 and 2015 increased in Poland by more than 50%. Regulation on the procedure for the adoption of the rules for the supply of water and the discharge of waste water may also give rise to reservations. On the basis of current legal solutions, the municipal council, following an analysis of the draft rules for water supply and sewage treatment developed by the water and sewage company, shall adopt rules for the supply of these media. The lack of sufficient inquisibility and determination in the control of the draft rules in question by the municipalities' councils, as well as the lack of knowledge by the councillors of legal and material matters in this respect, may result in the rules being adopted being contrary to the rules in force. Furthermore, competence in resolving disputes between water and sewage undertakings and recipients of services is a problematic issue. The wide range of tasks in the field of collective water supply and waste water discharge assigned to the municipality authority may give rise to a conflict of interest between the parties: the municipality, the enterprise and the recipient of services. In view of the above, the disadvantage of the functioning water and sewage market model is, in particular, that there is no possibility of external participation, a specialised independent regulatory authority in the field of collective water supply and sewage disposal tasks, in particular the tasks related to the verification and assessment of tariffs, and the question of the rules governing the provision of these media, as well as the settlement of disputes between service providers and recipients of these services. So three things: the first - the rules, the second case is the tariff, the third - the disputes. The proposed law provides for entrusting the function of the regulatory body to the director of the Regional Water Management Board of the State Water Farm of Poland. Consequently, the Director will be entrusted with the function of a legal body to safeguard the interests of the recipients of services. As I said, the exercise of competence in relation to tasks will concern in particular these three issues: tariffs, regulations and disputes. Since the Polish Waters will be established from the new year, until the entry into force of the Act - and the entry into force of this Act is designed still this year - it is proposed that the regulatory authority should be the National Water Management Board for a period of about 1.5 months. In addition, we provide that the responsibility of the Regional Director of the Water Management Board will be to collect and process information concerning water and sewage undertakings, including in particular the publication of information on average prices of water supply and waste water collection and the preparation and publication of reports on operating conditions. I would like to say to you that - so there is no doubt - each tariff, each tariff application will be dealt with for each municipality, for each company separately. This will not be the same as in part in the Energy Regulatory Office that there is, as it were, one tariff applicable in the region concerned. Each tariff application will be dealt with separately because each water and sewage undertaking has its own characteristics and each water and sewage undertaking has different pricing. These tariffs amount to around 2 and 3 thousand. on a national scale and here there will also be some novelty, namely the tariff will be fixed not for a year but for 3 years, since the period of 3 years shows in more detail the costs and expenses of these companies, as well as possible profits. The Supreme Audit Chamber pointed out that, if excessive profits were to arise in relation to the assumed margin, the tariff should be reduced in the following period. A very important issue with regard to transitional provisions: cases initiated and not completed before the date of entry into force of this Act are already subject to this Act, and if the municipality already has approved tariffs for next year, these tariffs will have to be verified by the water price regulator within 180 days. The draft law is compatible with European law. The draft amends three laws: the Act on collective water supply, the Act of 20 July 2017. Water law and the Act of 17 November 1964. Code of Civil Procedure. The appeal body for decisions in these cases, the first instance, will be the President of the National Management Board of Water Management of Polish Waters, and then, as far as judicial matters are concerned, litigation - competition court and consumers, I think that's what it's called. The proposed regulation will not have any financial consequences due to the fact that the regulator will operate within the budget of the proposed Polish Waters. This is an extremely important project for all citizens, for all residents using collective water supply and waste water discharge, i.e. for around 60% residents of Poland who benefit from this system. The point is that there should be no unjustified rise in water prices and waste water discharge, as indicated by the NIK report showing that this has been the case so far. It is not a law aimed at disrupting in some way the functioning of water supply and waste water collection systems, it is only intended to rationalise this so that users cover actual costs and that these costs are not excessive. I ask the High Sejm to pass this law. Thank you very much. [[Chairman of the proceedings includes Deputy Marshal Małgorzata Kidawa-Błońska Sejm]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u9	Thank you very much, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Richard Bartosik, Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u10	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club on the draft law amending the Act on collective supply of water and collective discharge of waste water and certain other laws. The presented project includes, among others, regulation on the supervision of the establishment of prices and charges for collective water supply and waste water discharge, as well as arrangements which will safeguard the interests of consumers of services provided by collective water supply and waste water discharge. The regulations introduced by this law will not impose additional charges on citizens or other public tributes. The draft law also sets out the terms and conditions for collective water supply intended for human consumption and for collective sewage disposal, including rules on the operation of water and sewage undertakings, the creation of conditions for ensuring continuity of supply and adequate water quality and reliable discharge and treatment of waste water, as well as the protection of the interests of customers of services, taking into account environmental protection and cost optimisation. The regulatory body will be the director of the Regional Water Management Board of the National Water Farm of Poland. The aim is to eliminate the risk of unjustified increases in prices and charges as well as in water collection and waste water collection prices. The regulatory body's task will be to assess draft rules for water supply and waste water discharge, to approve tariffs for collective water supply and for collective waste water discharge, and to resolve disputes between the water provider and service recipients, e.g. concerning the refusal of the conclusion of a water supply contract or the discharge of waste water by the undertaking, the cut-off of water supply or the closure of a sewage connection. Such decisions will be subject to appeal to the District Court in Warsaw - the competition court and consumers within 14 days. It will also be the task of the regulatory authority to impose fines for non-compliance with the obligation to submit tariffs for approval or to apply excessive rates. High Izbo! Water and sewage undertakings will be charged a fee for issuing a tariff approval decision. The company will also set a tariff for 3 years, which will allow the balancing of available water resources over a period of more than 1 year, which is important in the event of extreme weather events and will relieve companies from annual tariff setting tasks. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I ask you to continue to process the bill and to state that the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the draft law presented by the government. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u13	Thank you very much, Member. I invite Mr Józef Brynkus, the Kukiz Club15.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u14	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I present the position of the Kukiz club15 concerning the Act amending the Act on collective supply of water and collective discharge of waste water and certain other laws, print No 1905. In the case of any contested law, the question should be asked who will benefit from it. After examining the bill, the answer to this question is simple: no one. Moreover, all entities involved in the subject matter will suffer losses. The smallest, because image loss will be borne by the applicant, which is the government side, worse, that actual, real losses will be borne by Poles and Poles - that is to quote someone. The draft law concerns the task of the municipalities themselves, i.e. meeting the collective needs of territorial communities in the field of water supply and waste water collection. It leaves the municipalities full responsibility for this task, but deprives them of the most important tool - the right to determine tariffs for water and sewage charges, as well as transfers the power to formulate an opinion on the rules of water supply and the reception of waste water to the government institution, which is the state company of the Polish Waters. At the same time, the institution shall have no responsibility for carrying out this task. The units of local government are therefore becoming completely non-free. So far, the municipalities' councils have decided on the price of water and sewage, and they have done so quite well. Some argue, recalling concrete examples, that sometimes unjustified costs were included in the price of water and waste water, including, for example, personal or depreciation. The Ministry has solved this problem by introducing a provision that depreciation will be subject to all investments and not only to the part financed by own resources. So why spoil something that has generally worked well for years? So far, the locals have been able to account for their landlords and councillors for their fees for water and sewage. The government proposed a solution to the storm that has been established for many years the order of functioning of communal communities in Poland. After all, the element of this order was among others. the provision of adequate water and sewage infrastructure to residents and, where necessary, the adjustment of water and sewage charges, the grant of water and sewage charges from the revenues of the local government units themselves. The adjustment mechanism is also fatal, i.e. opinions on tariffs for water and sewage. Their prices in local environments will be influenced by a government official who is only driven by the average price of water and waste water in Poland, and who is completely unaware of the local realities of water extraction and waste water discharge. The effect of the proposed change will be to transfer certain amounts from the municipalities' budgets to the state budget and to aggravate their financial difficulties, but this will not benefit too much. The fees to be paid by local government units to the state company of the Polish Waters will hardly cover the costs of handling applications submitted by water and sewage companies. This is acknowledged in the explanatory memorandum of the bill even by its applicant. So what's the point of this change? It will result in the municipalities being forced to subsidise water prices to a greater extent than before, which will result in measures that could have been spent on other tasks disappearing. The absurd assumptions regarding the preparation of this law are reflected in a section of its justification. The designer states that the lack of sufficient inquisibility and determination during the control of the draft rules on water supply and sewage disposal by the municipalities' councils, as well as the lack of knowledge among the councillors of legal matters in this respect, led the regulations adopted to be contrary to the applicable rules. It simply proves that the applicant does not know the local reality of Poland. Probably never just part of it. This legislation cannot be improved and must therefore be rejected. On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I apply for the rejection of the draft law in print 1905 at first reading. I'd like the government side to come to their senses and withdraw this bad project. It will certainly bring her recognition among all, and it will benefit the citizens. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u16	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I am proud to present the position of the club vis-à-vis the government's draft law on amending the Act on collective supply of water and collective discharge of waste water and certain other laws. The municipalities discussed who would pay the grant, where the kindergarten was located in one municipality, and the host body or body that decided on the network was another municipality. The Act sets out the situation, i.e. it will be so that the grant will depend not on the location of the facility but on the operator and the registrant. Another grant is a scholarship grant. It gives poorer local governments a chance. The Act sets out an algorithm that allows local governments to propose a proportionally smaller contribution so that local authorities can benefit from this grant and, above all, to make grants to children, to the local community. I think this is a very important change, because it is a change for the good of children and for our comfort, because we will not be ashamed to admit that such subsidies are returning to the state budget. And the last grant for non-public or public facilities run by entities other than self-government. There are many changes in this area. These changes are a response to the demands of local government officials, because this grant caused many conflicts and even led to court proceedings. That is why the Act gave up the definition of the type of school, because nobody really knew what the concept of a school meant and the grants were charged in different ways. The grant will only be linked to a subsidy plus a specific indicator. In the case of these schools, ladies and gentlemen - and this is very important information - garbage contracts are also ending. The teacher will be employed in accordance with the Labour Code. The Act indicates that the grant will be settled in the case of registration in the educational information system, i.e. that only those entities who will be in the system by 30 September will be accounted for will indicate how many students have and how many teachers they employ. The Act also gives a lot of freedom to local authorities, who will be able to control at different times how the grant is implemented. It will be divided, will be paid in two tranches, of course, when the entity is registered in the educational information system. Then the grant will depend on the level of pay. Ladies and gentlemen, this is a very necessary bill, a law for which local government officials are waiting. I think the teachers are waiting for her, too. ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u98	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the report of the Joint Committee on Education, Science and Youth and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy on the Government's draft law on the financing of educational tasks. The draft law aims to create a coherent and transparent set of legislation on the scope of funding educational tasks. The project submitted concerns inter alia. pre-school grants, textbook grants, grants from the budgets of local government units, educational grants, financing of social benefits, as well as teachers' working time, promotion and evaluation of work, parental rights and leave. It is important to stress the importance of changes in the financing of social benefits which depend on the contribution of the municipality in this respect, depending on its financial condition. Municipalities that achieve a low income ratio will have to add 5% , and municipalities with an average income ratio of 10% instead of the current 20%. The reduction of the aid from the municipalities' own resources will increase access to this form of support. This is therefore a positive change. I consider it appropriate to update the provisions contained in the Teacher's Charter, which will be responsible for demographic and economic changes following the revision. The Act provides for an extension of the basic path of career promotion from 10 to 15 years, with the possibility of shortening it in case of outstanding job assessments. In addition, project designers want to harmonise the weekly time requirement, so-called. pence, teachers, educators, psychologists, speech counselors, pedagogical therapists and professional advisors up to 22 hours a week and teachers with special education qualifications, additionally employed to co-organise integration education and co-organize education for disabled pupils - up to 20 hours a week. The proposed law also introduces changes in the scope of leave for health punishment. An emergency subcommittee was set up to work on the clarification of the rules, involving the social side, and a number of amendments were tabled during the debate. October 10th this year The joint committees adopted the subcommittee report together with the amendments. The debate on the draft at the committee meeting took a very long time and some of the amendments were finally adopted. I am aware that not all the changes made are perfect, and I am convinced that they will not all respond. However, I know for a fact that we cannot continue to build on old, bad solutions that do not correspond to today's reality. The amendment of the bill meets social expectations. We have had a great deal of situations where the current rules raised interpretational doubts and caused many problems and ambiguities. I believe that the new provisions clearly regulate the scope of the law. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of the bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The analysis of Internet access carried out jointly by the Ministry of National Education and the Ministry of Digitalization in cooperation with the Office of Electronic Communications in 2016 showed that only 23% of Polish schools have access to Internet networks with a capacity of at least 100 Mbps. At the same time, it turned out that more than 40% educational units use the Internet with a capacity not exceeding 10 Mbps. The conclusions of this analysis are not optimistic, because the state described above means that most educational units in Poland are digitally excluded, which in the case of schools results in a lack of possibility to use digital educational tools in the teaching process. The fact that students are deprived of such opportunities means that they cannot acquire and develop digital competences and skills, and this has very far-reaching consequences - it is an obstacle to building the intellectual potential of the Polish society and to creating and maintaining an effective advantage of the Polish economy. The changes that have taken place in the global economy in recent years have been described as the fourth industrial revolution, in other words, the digital revolution. In order for Poland to participate as an equal partner, and even as a leader, it is crucial to allow access to new technologies, because, as the old proverb says: What John has not learned, John will not be able to do. Therefore, today's draft law on the National Education Network is a necessary project that meets the expectations of many environments. The National Education Network will be a public telecommunications network through which the operator - the Scientific and Academic Computer Network - will provide basic and non-basic schools with broadband Internet access services, but also, which is extremely important, will provide widely understood network security to its users. Importantly, access to a basic service, i.e. one with a capacity of up to 100 Mbps, will be free for educational establishments and, as it is understood, for bodies running schools. The funding for the functioning of the OSE will come from the state budget, but it is worth noting that it is planned to reach for the funds from the Operational Programme of Poland digital. Many factors determine the success of each undertaking, but it must be made clear that money is always fundamental. The Act sets the maximum limit of the appropriations provided for in the State budget for the implementation of tasks related to the creation and maintenance of the National Education Network. Between 2018 and 2027 the total amount of PLN 1341 million will be allocated. Dear Marshal, High Seym! Therefore, we have a diagnosis and therapeutic measures have also been indicated. It is worth asking here what the effects of these changes will be. And they will be of all measure positive. I will not mention them all, because they can be found in the justification, but I will point out only one fundamental effect: equalizing the educational opportunities of Polish students both in large cities and in small centres, which are located in poorly urbanized areas. This is very important, very important. Ladies and gentlemen! The guidelines for the proposed solutions can be described as very good, and this is supported by the fact that similar solutions have been introduced in many countries around the world. It is enough to mention Germany, Japan, Denmark or Ireland. In view of the above, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the proposed bill on the National Education Network. [[♪ The bell ♪]] However, we do see the need for minor changes, and I would therefore like to make four amendments to the bill to the hands of the Marshal. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u171	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party our position on the report of the committee on the government draft law on the National Education Network. The aim of this project is to link all schools into a nationwide ICT network, which will provide an Internet access service together with network and ICT security services, as well as ICT tools supporting the learning process towards a digital model. The Polish Education Network (OSE) will be a virtual public telecommunications network based on physical broadband infrastructure. This network will be operated by the entity responsible for launching and maintaining the network and, in particular, for providing broadband services to schools with a symmetrical capacity of at least 100 Mbps together with network security services. There is also an OSE portal maintained by the operator. It will contain a service part, e.g. to report problems, and will enable the participants in the educational process to cooperate. In addition, a platform will be set up to present educational resources that will be created by providers of educational content, including public and educational institutions, universities, scientific and research institutes, educational institutions, NGOs and commercial companies. Importantly, the cost of the programme will remain on the state side. Currently 5,8,000. the location of schools is covered by commercial investment plans of telecommunication undertakings. In these locations, operators will build the network with their own resources. Over 8,000. the location of schools is located in areas where applications for funding were submitted in the framework of the second competition in the first axis of the Operational Programme. The beneficiaries will be required to connect all schools in the areas where they will carry out projects. Other schools that do not have access to high-speed internet will be connected in the framework of the next competitions of the first axis of the Operational Programme. If there remain schools that have not been connected to commercial investments and investments financed by PO PCs, they will be connected by the OSE operator. High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u192	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Dear Minister, On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I am pleased to present my position on the report of the Committee on Public Finance on the Government's draft law amending the excise duty Act and the Act on the organisation of certain agricultural markets, the original print No. 1865. As a result of the amendment of the excise duty law introduced from 1 January 2016 and as a result of other actions, the fall in the grey zone in the cigarette market has already been observed. The draft law contained in the committee's report 1926 is in line with the Government's submission, which introduced several legislative amendments during the committee's work, and is intended to seal the tax system in the tobacco industry. The tobacco industry is crucial for the state budget due to the high share of excise duty on tobacco products in the overall budgetary impact. In 2016, the State budget for excise duties on tobacco products received revenue of PLN 18,5 billion, which represents an amount of PLN 700 million higher than in 2015. Reinforcing control services should also include more effective control of tobacco drought. The illicit trade in tobacco products so far is yet another of the most profitable spheres of the grey zone. The proposed amendments to the Excise Tax Act aim to seal the tax system for the trading of tobacco drought and to facilitate the pursuit of business activities for legal operators trading in tobacco drought. It is also proposed to allow the lodging of excise security by a third party to facilitate the conduct of business to operators trading in tobacco droughts. In line with the demands of the intermediaries, the upper limit of the flat-rate security lodged by these operators is expected to be reduced from PLN 30 million to PLN 15 million. The Act also introduces another form of excise security, which is a mortgage on real estate. This amendment meets the demands of intermediary tobacco operators in this respect. The amendments to the Act on the organisation of certain agricultural markets aim to seal the marketing of dried tobacco leaves, hereinafter referred to as tobacco raw materials, in Poland. Monitoring of tobacco cultivation and the production and disposal of raw tobacco, as well as control of tobacco cultivation and the production and disposal of raw tobacco are introduced. Ww. monitoring and control are aimed at limiting the supply of raw tobacco to illegally operating tobacco factories. The draft law introduces rules on penalties for non-compliance with the provisions contained in the draft, in order to ensure the reliability of the reported data for monitoring tobacco cultivation in view of the need to limit the supply of raw tobacco to tobacco-producing establishments operating without appropriate registration and not applying the taxes due to the State budget. The proposed solutions actually impose a number of obligations, but are very beneficial for the tobacco industry itself. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the report on the government draft law amending the excise duty law and the law on the organisation of certain agricultural markets included in the sejm print No. 1926. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The proposed law also adds the provision that buyers of raw tobacco, i.e. intermediary tobacco operators and foreign companies with branches established in the territory of the country, are required to provide the Director-General of the National Agricultural Support Centre with data on the mass of raw tobacco collected by group of varieties. However, this provision is problematic. As indicated during the consultation, the introduction of strict penalties for infringement of the proposed law in the form of non-disclosure, delayed transmission of information, incomplete or non-true information should be postponed in time after a sufficiently long and effective information campaign has been carried out among tobacco growers. By imposing additional burdens on tobacco growers, they can be effectively discouraged from further production, especially given that tobacco cultivation is currently not so profitable. During the committee's work, all our doubts were not resolved and, unfortunately, our suggestions were not taken into account. However, bearing in mind that the sealing of the tax system in the field of trade in tobacco drought is a very important issue for the state budget, and the Modern one is committed to making life easier for entrepreneurs and not hindering it through further administrative burdens, we recommend this draft law. Thank you very much, Marshal.

The project contains few changes for tobacco traders. In fact, the only positive change is the reduction of the upper limit of the flat-rate security lodged by the intermediary tobacco operators or by entities representing the foreign trader from the current PLN 30 million to PLN 15 million and the possibility of setting up an excise security in the form of a mortgage on immovable property, as well as the possibility that the required excise security may also be lodged by third parties. There were significant irregularities in trade in tobacco droughts before 1 January 2016 in terms of fictitious sale or sale of tobacco dried without excise duty to unauthorised persons. The sale of dried tobacco to another intermediary tobacco operator has not been documented. The amendment to the excise duty Act as of 1 January 2016 however eliminated these irregularities and today the project under discussion seems to be a certain overzealous, unnecessary, a manifestation of purely bureaucratic actions. ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u214	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present the report of the Committee on Public Finance, print No. 1925, on the government bill amending the Act on Local Taxes and Charges, print No. 1877. The Sejm at its 49th meeting on 11 October, in accordance with the Rule. 39 (1) (a) 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the above draft law to the Committee on Public Finances for consideration. The Public Finance Commission, after considering the above draft law at its meeting on 13 October 2017, requests the adoption of the draft law in its entirety. Three articles were discussed at the committee meeting, the committee recommended their adoption and recommended the adoption of the draft law in its entirety. In short, in order to say what it is about: the draft law amending the law on local taxes and charges aims to introduce an obligation for tax authorities to report annually on property tax, agricultural tax and forestry tax to the Minister responsible for public finances selected information on these taxes. Until now, the Ministry of Finance has collected data on real estate, agricultural and forestry taxes by means of voluntary surveys of municipalities carried out at intervals of several years. At the moment, the aim is to improve the conduct of sound tax policies, in particular the design of changes in the property taxation system, including the estimation of the effects of the proposed changes. To this end, it is appropriate to introduce in the Act on local taxes and charges amendments to the rules imposing on the tax authorities of the municipalities an obligation to draw up a report on these three previously mentioned taxes. It should also be added that these changes were accepted and were also requested by the local government. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u226	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The reports will be transmitted electronically to the Minister responsible for public finances through the Regional Financial Chambers. So far, the Ministry of Finance has only had access to data on the budgetary revenues of these taxes and on the consequences of reliefs and exemptions. After the amendment of the law, the Ministry will gain access to data on the tax base of individual taxes, areas or exemptions from charges on the basis of decisions of the municipality council, as well as to current tax rates in individual municipalities. So far, data on tax rates have been collected by means of surveys which did not give a complete picture of the tax policy pursued in individual municipalities. I believe that the amendment of the law will significantly contribute to increasing the scope for sound tax policy. The deadline for preparing the report has been adjusted by the projectors to the deadline by which the relevant tax authorities will have access to the information needed to submit the report. The proposed regulation does not entail an increase in expenditure or a reduction in the income of financial sector entities. In my view, the preparation of the annual report will not be too much of a burden for the employees of the tax authorities who will be responsible for preparing the report, especially in relation to the benefits that this report will bring. The amendment of the Act will increase the scope for analysing existing tax arrangements, facilitate the assessment of the structure of revenues broken down by type of tax entity and the determination of average rates of taxation in individual municipalities. This in turn will allow for a more rational tax policy of the government, which will benefit all of us. About Free and Solidarnia both before and now supports the bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u264	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to present the position of the club regarding the government draft law amending the Law on Road Traffic and some other laws. So far, there have been no provisions in Polish legislation that allow the so-called. professional vehicle registration, which would simplify the activities of vehicle distribution, production or testing operators and enable testing vehicles to be carried out in the course of their activities. The professional registration of vehicles would entitle the operator involved in the activity, the authorised body or the test body of the vehicle manufacturer to use the professional registration certificate and the professional registration plates for the purpose of carrying out the test driving of vehicles in the course of the activities carried out by those operators. The package of professional vehicle registration requirements proposed in this project is an effective legal tool to reduce the administrative burden while at the same time serving the legal and safe use of vehicles. The proposed law will allow testing vehicles to be carried out in the course of their activities by entities involved in the distribution, production and testing of vehicles. It should be noted that professional registrations together with professional registration plates will allow an authorised trader or an authorised entity to use many vehicles on the road for the purpose of their business activity in the field of distribution, production or testing of vehicles. Professional vehicle registration, which is enjoyable, applies not only to new vehicles, but also to second-hand vehicles, including previously registered or imported from abroad. I would like to point out that such solutions are applied by other Member States and that the absence of such solutions was unjustified. It caused Polish vehicle distributors to be in a worse position than their colleagues in other countries. A significant change in the proposed law should also be noted. According to the existing regulations, a person interested in buying a vehicle is not entitled to drive in person to check the vehicle before it is acquired. The project provides for this possibility for the future buyer of the vehicle. This possibility will also exist for vehicles imported from abroad. Attention should also be paid to the changes in the above mentioned. new laws concerning Code of Offences, Road Transport Act, Mandatory Insurance Act. The good thing is that there's an emergency rule in the article. 5, with a view to providing sufficient time for the preparation of an electronic solution and the determination of the conduct by the holder, in paper or electronic form, of the list of commercial vehicles, i.e. not more than 24 months from the date of entry into force of the proposed law. Positively, the date of entry into force of the Act should be assessed: 18 months after the date of publication. In determining this deadline, account has been taken of the need to provide sufficient time for the adoption of a package of implementing rules. ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u274	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the government draft law amending the Law on Road Traffic and some other laws. The amendment of the Road Traffic Law introduces a new form of temporary registration of vehicles. It will enable operators involved in the distribution, production and testing of vehicles to carry out tests without having to register any tested vehicle at the office. The regulations included in the project will simplify the activities of companies in the automotive industry, relieving them from many administrative obligations, while ensuring that vehicles can be used legally in road traffic. Today, an automotive dealership, a commission, but also an institution that tests cars must register each such vehicle before leaving for public roads. It generates a lot of costs and takes a lot of time. Therefore, for a long time the automotive industry has been calling for the introduction of the so-called. professional license plates. The proposed solutions address these problems. According to the draft law, a document stating the entry into service of a vehicle, moped, agricultural tractor or trailer will be a professional registration document with professional registration plates for the purpose of carrying out the test drive. Professional registration document and professional registration plates will be able to use entrepreneurs with their registered office or branch in Poland involved in the production, distribution or testing of vehicles, as well as authorized entities, e.g. Auto Transport Institute or manufacturer's research units. It was assumed that the right-holders, especially those involved in distribution, would be able to use the possibility of professional registration of vehicles not only for new vehicles but also used, including previously registered vehicles or imported from abroad. The professional registration will confirm the right to carry out road tests, which is extremely important, the vehicle subject to professional registration will be able to be operated not only by the authorized entity or its employee, but also by the customer or the hired driver. The current provisions provide that test driving can be carried out in practice only by the holder and the potential buyer is an accompanying person. According to the project, the body carrying out this professional registration will be an old-timer. The decision to issue evidence after the entity has lodged an authorised administrative fee will issue an old-timer, issuing the blanks and registration plates. Such registration will be valid for a year. This will also provide a source of income for the district government, because if the entrepreneur does not return used and unused blanks of professional registration and license plates, he will be obliged to pay a penalty of up to 3,000. zł, while for not keeping a list of vehicles in use, a penalty of up to 5 000 will be liable. zł. Punishment will be imposed by the governor, and the revenue will go to the county budget. The new rules will also allow the collection of relevant data in the central vehicle register and allow for effective verification of the professional registration of vehicles by road control authorities. The holder is to keep a list of vehicles used by him in road traffic. The list will be carried out in an electronic system which cooperates with central vehicle registers in this respect. The Ministry of Digitalization is to make available to authorised entities a free web application by which they will enter data on vehicles used under professional registration into the central vehicle register and thus keep a list at central level. It's a very good change. It will also facilitate checks on the use of vehicles which may come from different sources, not necessarily operating legally. So here we also have some control over the use of vehicles that are traded. The proposed changes to the legislation provide companies involved in the trade and testing of vehicles with ease of operation by not having to register them in standard mode. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! It is a very good and long-awaited bill, especially by entrepreneurs from the automotive industry. The Free and Solidarnia Members' Circle will support this bill. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

I ask Mrs Józef Hrynkiewicz to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u284	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, I have the honour to present the position of the committee on the government draft law amending the Act on Civil Register together with draft implementing acts, print No 1815 of the Polish Parliament of the 8th term of office. The proposed law amends the Act of 24 September 2010 on population records. The main objective of the proposed amendment to the Civil Records Act is to repeal the provisions which abolished the reporting obligation. The decision to abolish the reporting obligation was taken in 2008. This was reflected in the 2010 Act. The amendment introduced had a long vacathio legis, therefore the essential changes concerning registration of the address of the population were introduced by the Law of 7 December 2012 in the Act amending the Act on the Register of Population and Personal Evidence. This law entered into force on 1 January 2013. The Act introduced on 1 January 2013 liberalised the reporting obligation and introduced a very extensive catalogue of amendments to the Act. However, these changes have not developed a solution to the compulsory registration of the place of residence. It was therefore necessary to start work adapting the implementation of statutory amendments. The introduction of the Act also involved the cessation of the collection of reporting data in one central register of PESEL established in the 1980s. The data for this system were and continue to be obtained by municipalities authorities mainly from persons fulfilling the reporting obligation. The introduction of the reporting changes and the need to coordinate the work of the Ministry on the implementation of the changes resulted in the appointment by the President of the Council of Ministers on 13 June 2014 under the chairmanship of the Minister for the Interior of the Interministerial Board of Reporters composed of representatives of the National Office of Elections, the Chief Data Protection Officer, the Office for Foreigners, Municipalities and other institutions. As a result of the team's work, it was concluded that for the proper execution of constitutional and statutory tasks and duties... [[Game in the O.R.]] Mr. Marshal! I am very sorry, but I am very disturbed by Mr Grabarczyk. As a result of the team's work, it was concluded that the collection of data on the place of residence in one reference PESEL register is essential for the proper execution of constitutional and statutory tasks and public administration duties vis-à-vis citizens. This also has positive and beneficial effects for citizens themselves, including: in the implementation of electoral law or in the smooth execution of social security tasks. Information on the number and structure of residents living in a given administrative unit is necessary for local authorities that can carry out the statutory tasks in the field of care, education, social assistance or disaster relief. Also drawing up electoral registers, calculation e.g. the number of aldermen, conducting referendums requires information about the number and structure of residents of the commune, district or voivodship. The data on residents are also the basis for calculating subsidies, grants and determining the amount of capital funds. The administration has a duty to deal with a lot of citizens' matters because of their local jurisdiction, as evidenced by the place where the citizen is checked in. Information about the person's whereabouts is also needed in case of events of chance, events related to crisis management, medical rescue, sanitary protection or other circumstances of chance. Registration records are also important in matters of internal security for citizens of the State. The security aspect of citizens has become the subject of a public debate in the European Union in recent years. It is particularly important in the intensified fight against terrorist threats. The reporting obligation exists in the vast majority of EU countries. The contested law introduces a simplified reporting obligation, as well as ordering and clarifying certain issues relating to it. The Act introduces the obligation to check-in and check-out, to apply for a trip abroad and to return from abroad in accordance with the provisions of the Act. The reporting obligation may be carried out by electronic means. The provisions of the Act allow the possibility to register in the form of an electronic document. This form will significantly facilitate the fulfilment of this obligation. This solution is in line with the current principle that every citizen should be able to handle by electronic means any matter in contact with the administration concerned. Check-in by electronic means is also possible via a proxy. The Act sets out in detail what rules and in what form the check-in can be made. The Act is accompanied by implementing acts and model forms for registrations as clearly as possible describing the procedure for check-in and check-out at the place of stay. The Act also introduces changes to the registration system for foreigners. According to the project, on the basis of an in-depth analysis of existing alien registers, changes are made to remove existing alien residence registers and collect data in the central register of PESEL. This involves giving foreigners PESEL numbers and collecting data in this system. The PESEL number will not be given to foreigners whose stay in Poland is short-term or only transit. The PESEL number will be transmitted in case of a stay longer than 3 months. To date, data collected about foreigners will not be lost, but archived in accordance with the principle. The project clarifies the procedure for the implementation of the reporting obligation by citizens of the European Union. It is a novelty to introduce the possibility of obtaining a PESEL number at the same time when applying for an identity card for people residing in Poland, which significantly simplifies and accelerates the procedure for obtaining an identity card. It appears from the materials provided to the Sejm that the draft law was consulted with all the institutions and organisations concerned. The results of the consultation and the conclusions of these consultations are attached to Parliament's printing No 1815, so that Members can learn the content of the consultation and the positions taken by the various parties. The position of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs expressed in the discussion and in the vote was almost unambiguously positive. In the vote, only one of the members of the committee abstained from voting, the other members were in favour of the bill. The deadline for the entry into force of the Act is 1 January 2018. This deadline, taking into account the completeness of the Act and the completeness of the draft implementing acts which did not raise objections to the members of the Board of Administration and Home Affairs, the committee considered it realistic and did not raise objections, as no objections were raised as to the content of the Act and the solutions proposed therein, and no comments were made on the content of the implementing acts. The Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, after discussion and after evaluation of the draft law, together with the accompanying implementing acts, recommends that the High House adopt a law without amendments. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u285	Thank you very much, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. As the first Mrs Józef Hrynkiewicz, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice. I think the Member has already said everything.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u286	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour on behalf of the club Law and Justice to present the position of the club on the bill amending the law on population records. The club, which I would like to point out at the beginning, will vote in accordance with the recommendation of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs - in favour of adopting the draft law without amendment. At the same time, on behalf of the club, I would like to stress the great importance of this law to improve the quality of government in the country. Reportable information, and therefore related demographic information, is today one of the most important reasons for the relevance of social and economic decisions taken by public authorities, both by the government and by local governments at all levels. Demographic information gives objective reasons e.g. an appropriate placement of investments in infrastructure for social and economic tasks. The changes made are of great importance to public statistics, and especially to the study of demographic processes and phenomena which are currently the basis for all serious social and economic analyses, as well as the proposed changes in the country, and good, reliable public statistics is, after all, the basis for good governance. The registration records will allow for systematic observation and statistical surveys of the processes of natural movement, and in particular of the wandering movement, i.e. migration of the population. Observations of the migration processes of the population with simultaneous analyses of the structure and size of migration are an important prerequisite and basic data for assessing important social and economic processes. They will be important indications as regards the design of changes in living conditions and the development of individual administrative units. Observation of migration processes will assess their impact and contribute to making better decisions on many infrastructure and economic projects. Poland has been an emigration country for many years, and so far there is no efficient system for registering population flows. In demographic forecasts on migration, we use an estimation of the size of processes or foreign registers, which do not always give us the information we want in population analysis. Having their own registers will not only improve analytical processes and research on this important issue, but will also significantly increase their cognitive value and relevance and usefulness. We will therefore vote in favour of the adoption and implementation of this law.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u287	Thank you very much. Mr Marek Wójcik, the Civic Platform Club, will now speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u288	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! It is worth recalling that the reporting obligation was introduced by the Act of 14 July 1961 on the registration and control of the movement of the population. And it was a law, which, from our perspective, was indeed a very restrictive law imposing numerous obligations on citizens. For 50 years, practically reporting was not subject to any changes. Let me remind you that in order to fulfil the reporting obligation, it was necessary for example. present the current military book, provide various types of data, the reporting obligation had to be respected within 4 days and the failure to report was punishable by criminal sanctions. The fulfilment of this obligation has been linked to numerous restrictions and difficulties for the population. In 2008 it was decided to abolish the reporting obligation with full awareness that it would be necessary to develop a formula for powering public registers so that the state administration and local government administration could simply function normally, also for the purposes of public statistics, which Prof. Hrynkiewicz spoke a lot about. Surely such registration of the place of residence would be necessary. In 2010, the Act was able to abolish the reporting obligation, while creating a very long period of vacathio legis, in which time solutions were to be developed so that all those objectives related to the functioning of the administration and the objectives of public statistics, the holding of elections, etc. could be achieved. During this work a number of analyses have been carried out and studies have also been carried out on how to simplify these procedures related to compliance with the reporting obligation so that they are not actually as burdensome for the population as those which were properly introduced. 50 years earlier. And the Law of 7 December 2012 managed to eliminate most of these nuisances for residents. I would like to remind you that this was a law which introduced the abolition of the registration obligation for holidaymakers and tourists, introduced the possibility of the registration obligation by the proxy, introduced the possibility of simultaneous check-out at check-in in a new place, created a completely different, more realistic, 30-day deadline for fulfilling the registration obligation. As I mentioned, the previous deadline was four days. Criminal sanctions were lifted for failure to comply with the reporting obligation, and the obligation to submit various types of documents, such as the military book, or to provide various data, were waived when the reporting obligation was fulfilled. It can be said that from the entry into force of the Act, i.e. from 1 January 2013, to the old reporting obligation, the one that was created in the 1960s, there was actually no trace. It can be said that all these nuisances for citizens were simply abolished by the 2012 Act, and after the former reporting obligation of the 1960s it was actually only the name - a name that was not changed. In addition, you are going in a very similar way in the government project because you are introducing further facilitations for the population in relation to the fulfilment of the reporting obligation. This is, for example. Check-in by electronic means. I think it's a step in the right direction. Indeed, technological development has led to the possibility of thinking also about electronic compliance with the reporting obligation. This is a very good change. We therefore believe that today's reporting obligation is indeed not burdensome for citizens. We also believe that in this situation, in order not to make further changes, not to create a situation in which a new system for registering citizens' residence would still have to be created in the future, the solutions proposed by the Act are rational. That is why we are in favour of passing the bill for further work. Thank you very much.

There are also some legal provisions that make it dependent on how many inhabitants there are in a given region to give certain powers to local authorities. As regards the benefits, it would be worth noting that the actual abolition of the reporting obligation would require the obligation to apply to other registers. In this arrangement, these registers will not be necessary. Another example - in cities there are free parking zones for residents. At a time when we don't have a record of who lives there, it's hard to decide whether one of the residents should be parked free of charge here or not. Therefore, as you can see, life has shown that such a clear decision to delete the reporting obligation has encountered so many obstacles that it seems that the decision to restore is as correct as possible, and I can declare on behalf of the Modern Club that the Modern Club will support the solutions contained in the government submission. ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u291	Thank you very much. Mr Zbigniew Sosnowski, the club of the Polish People's Party, will now speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u292	Dear Marshal, Dear Minister, High Izbo! The reporting obligation was introduced in Poland in 1928. After World War II, the obligation to register the place of residence was included in the Act of 14 July 1961, and another regulation in the field of reporting was reflected in the Act of 10 April 1974 on population records and personal evidence. In response to citizens' expectations regarding freedom of movement, the legislator abolished the reporting obligation in 2008. The current geopolitical situation, however, calls for reflection, given the broad sense of security. In deciding to restore the reporting obligation, it is proposed to Polish citizens to introduce further simplifications in the performance of the reporting obligation. Today's regulation will make it possible to carry out all activities relating to the obligation to report electronically, which should be very easy for citizens, but also for the staff of the office. The amendments introduced by these regulations aim to introduce the principle of giving the number PESEL to foreigners who register for permanent or temporary stay in the territory of the Republic of Poland, and thus to remove the registers of residence of foreigners. These registers were necessary when the numbers of PESEL were not given to foreigners. The collection of data on the place of residence of citizens in a single reference PESEL register results in many benefits for themselves, for example. the possibility of voting in elections without the need to apply for inclusion in electoral lists, the efficient service of citizens in the field of social security, i.e. pensions, pensions, social welfare or social security matters. It should be mentioned that knowledge of the number and structure of the population and data resulting from only one reference register will allow local authorities to take more effective and effective action in this area. At this point, it is appropriate to mention that knowledge of the number of inhabitants has an impact on the calculation of the number of councillors and is also important in the context of the local referendum. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Rapid and effective access to the PESEL reference register is essential in matters of fundamental state defence, as well as in the search for persons for trial and missing persons. It should also be mentioned that knowledge of the whereabouts of a person is essential in the course of crisis management activities or emergency services. It should be fully accepted that the absence of an address in the PESEL register may cause difficulties in establishing and reaching persons whose life and health are at risk because of the suspicion that they are present in the disaster area. Similarly, the European Union's acquis, which is regulated in the fight against terrorism, cannot be effectively implemented in the case of very loose or inoperative regulations in the individual Member States of the European Union concerning the reporting obligation of citizens residing in the territory concerned. Faced with these facilitations and, at the same time, taking into account the reasons envisaged for the benefits for citizens, as well as the importance of security issues, it is appropriate to agree that this regulation will satisfy citizens' expectations and bring the state's rights together. The Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party raises no objections to the detailed solutions contained in the draft law and will vote in favour of the committee's report. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u314	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position on the government's draft law on amending the Act on Civil Records. There are two issues in the revised draft. First, the government departs from the provisions abolishing from 1 January 2018 so-called. reporting obligation. Over the years, the reporting obligation has ceased to be burdensome for citizens, but at the same time its existence ensures that the fundamental rights and obligations of Polish law are fulfilled. Here there are issues related to electoral rights, the right to court or the protection of citizens in the event of a threat. It is worth noting that the reporting obligation is not restrictive in Poland, unlike many other European countries. The amendment also assumes that it will be possible to check in online, which is a real ease. I am very pleased that the government notes the digitalisation of society and introduces such a major change in the adjustment of people's responsibilities to the modern world. This is actually a very real relief. Another simplification is the introduction of a single form for a person who checks in for a permanent stay and at the same time wants to check out of a temporary stay. The same solution is intended for people who check in for a temporary stay and at the same time want to check out of a permanent stay. The second group of changes is linked to the registers of foreigners. According to the project, the registers of foreigners operating today in municipalities are being wound up. As a result, all foreigners will be given PESEL numbers, and their data instead of e.g. In three registers, they'll only be in one. This solution will allow rapid access to all alien data in one central and easily searched register accessible to authorised services and administrations. This protection allows better security for Polish citizens. The draft law has generated positive reactions to all policy options among Members. That's her best recommendation. However, I agree with the designers that a change of name would result in too much burden on the state budget. Changing this bill is a step in the right direction. In addition, it introduces a single amendment to the Law of 29 July 2005 on the Prevention of Drug Addiction and the Act of 9 July 1995 on the Protection of Health against the Consequences of Tobacco and Tobacco Products. The main objective of the proposed revision of the Law of 19 August 1994 on the protection of mental health is to adapt it to the changed legal conditions resulting from decisions of the European Court of Human Rights and the Constitutional Tribunal. The contested law also includes the amendment of certain regulations concerning the application of direct coercion or the financing of health services in the field of psychiatric health care. A very important element of the proposed amendment is also the revision of the regulations concerning the Centre for Mental Health. I would like to stress that changes are being made in the project, which are of a sequencing nature. The project also includes amendments to the provisions of the Law on the coordination of the National Mental Health Programme and the obligation to inform about its implementation, to strengthen the role of the Minister responsible for Health in the implementation of the National Mental Health Programme and to extend and clarify the issues concerning the Centre for Mental Health currently regulated in Article. 5a of the Act, the modification of the statutory authorisations for the issuing of implementing acts - as mentioned above, their purpose is to amend these authorisations, to repeal or transfer the content covered by the implementing act directly to the Act due to the matter they regulate - as well as in terms of terminology. The amendment also proposes a change in the way the municipal committees operate. to solve alcoholic problems which will enable them to function in a manner consistent with the provisions of the Law of 28 August 1997. High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! The proposed amendments to this law are expected and justified. Through the provisions in the Act we improve the area of mental health care. ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u326	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The main objective of the proposed amendment to the Law of 19 August 1994 on the protection of mental health is to adapt it to the changed legal conditions resulting from the judgments of the European Court of Human Rights and the Constitutional Tribunal. In accordance with the amendment to the adoption of a person's social assistance home without his or her consent, and with the consent of his or her statutory representative, a decision by the guardian court to refer him or her to such an establishment will be required. The new rules regulate the obligation to periodically examine the mental health of a person in such a home at least once every six months. The time shown seems to allow a proper assessment of the health status of a person's stay in such a facility. In addition, a person who does not agree to a further stay in a social assistance home will be able to request the abolition of the obligation to stay there. In order to protect the legal person who is directed against his will to a psychiatric hospital or a social assistance centre, a lawyer or counsel shall be required to be appointed by law. A record of the need to monitor the premises of the persons to whom a measure of direct coercion in the form of isolation has been applied has been introduced. Monitoring is designed to increase patient safety and allow for more accurate control of their condition. In addition, the project is clear and orderly. The amendment of the Law of 26 October 1982 on sobriety education and the prevention of alcoholism is linked to the need to introduce changes in the possibility of applying to the court for a change in the type of rehab facility. There will also be a provision for monitoring the premises in which persons with direct isolation coercion measures are held will change the functioning of the municipal alcoholic problem resolution committees. It is proposed to repeal the Article. 20 of this law, which concerns the obligation to submit information on the implementation of this law by the Council of Ministers to the Sejm of the Republic of Poland annually. This obligation is enshrined in the Public Health Act. This will reduce the duplication of information obligations. The judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal requires the amendment of the Act of 29 July 2005 on the Prevention of Drug Addiction and the Act of 9 July 1995 on the Protection of Health against the Consequences of Tobacco and Tobacco Products. They propose applying the same rules as will be introduced in the Law of 26 October 1982 on sobriety and combating alcoholism. The amendment aims to increase the powers of the persons affected by the amended laws, in particular by guaranteeing the protection of procedural rights, as well as the protection of the rights of persons led without their consent to welfare homes and those subject to direct coercion. The implementation of the National Mental Health Programme will be improved by rationalising the reporting obligations imposed on the authorities set out in the Mental Health Act and the Law on Sobriety Education and Against Alcoholism. This will ensure systemic consistency with the Public Health Act. The amendments will make it possible to transfer the provisions of certain implementing acts to the law and to increase the legal certainty of persons whose obligations were derived from the provisions of a lower rank, such as regulations, by eliminating existing legal doubts. The Polish People's Party will support the above-mentioned law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u336	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position on the government proposal for a law amending the Law on the Protection of Mental Health and some other laws. The amendment discussed today includes, among others, adapts the Polish law to the judgments of the European Court of Human Rights and the Constitutional Tribunal. The regulations contained in the project cover many aspects of broadly understood mental health. A large part is devoted to security and clarifies the provisions involved. Among other things, it introduces an obligation to set up a lawyer or counsel and mandatory periodic mental health examinations of persons to the extent that they are justified by their stay in a social assistance home. It is a solution that increases safety and protects people who are not self-conscious and so obvious that it is surprising that they are only being introduced now. In addition, the new rules prevent a person from being sent to a social security home without his consent or an appropriate custody court ruling. The person concerned, his family or the head of the establishment where he or she is or is referred to, may request a change in the decision of acceptance or the removal of the obligation to stay in the DPS and medicinal entities after the new rules have been established. In view of the cases cited in the explanatory memorandum of the European Court of Human Rights and the Constitutional Tribunal, I consider it appropriate and necessary to introduce the proposed solutions. These changes will increase the security and legal protection of persons subject to direct coercion, thereby contributing to the elimination of cases of abuse and the unjustified use of this instrument. I also welcome the establishment of the Minister of Health as coordinator of the National Mental Health Programme and the new provisions imposing an obligation to submit information on the implementation of this programme every 2 years. I agree with the argument that the extension of this period will allow for a fuller analysis of the information collected and will provide an opportunity to develop more effective solutions to identified and properly defined problems. The great advantage of the amendment, which has long been expected, is the introduction of regulations to regulate the status of municipal alcoholic problem resolution committees. Finally, a change in the way they operate will enable them to act in a way that is consistent with the rules, in particular the provisions of the Act of 28 August 1997 on the protection of personal data. I believe that the elimination of existing legal doubts will benefit both the actions of the members of the committee and the persons under their supervision, which will consequently benefit patients and increase the effectiveness of treatment. In my opinion, a major flaw is the narrowing down of the legislators' actions to amend the rules related to alcohol addiction. I believe that, in the context of this novel, it was necessary to lean on the municipal committees to solve alcohol problems and turn them into municipal committees to solve addiction problems. It is a misunderstanding, in my view, to remove signs of prohibition on the sale, administration or consumption of alcoholic beverages on ships and at sea ports. In the year that the High Sejm has adopted a resolution for the year of increasing concern for the sobriety of the nation, we increase the number of places where alcohol will be available. I believe that we should still discuss this amendment at the meeting of the Committee on Health, in time for those dealing with addiction issues to take part in it. However, I know that sending the project back to the committee will prevent the rapid entry into force of other important and expected changes. Thus, taking into account these reservations and taking into account above all the well-being of patients, and hoping that the bad rules will be corrected in a completely new law on sobriety education and combating alcoholism, near Free and Solidarni will support the procedated bill.
