ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u340	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Ladies and gentlemen! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the government's draft law on basic health care. Fundamental healthcare plays a key role in the health system. The experience of other countries shows that developed and organised basic health care is able to satisfy up to 90% the health needs of the population. In Poland, POZ should also be the foundation of the health care system. He's predisposed to it, and he needs the potential to do it. However, there is a lack of proper regulation. It is therefore necessary to redefine the role and place of primary health care in the health system. The proposed law will strengthen this part of the system and create the conditions for improving the efficiency of primary healthcare. The benefits for citizens include, among others, improve the satisfaction of the care provided and improve the quality and safety of the benefits provided. One of the priorities is to put in place solutions for improving the quality of benefits and to create mechanisms for monitoring this quality. In particular, it is important to improve the coordination of patient care, so the key objective of the reform is to establish a core healthcare team that will take care of each patient. This team will include a doctor, nurse and midwife. The team's work will be coordinated by a doctor who will determine how and how to perform diagnostic and therapeutic procedures. Thanks to this reform, we will create mechanisms for continuous improvement of the quality and safety of the benefits provided, as well as the organisation and functioning of the whole system. The patient care system has been fragmented, and today every unit treats the patient for a different disease and on a different basis. That is why we want to restore the coordination of health care with a patient whose primary health care will be a pillar. The new POZ, like European, Western European, is largely based on family medicine, but we do not close ourselves to other specializations. These changes require preparation, doctors are needed for this specialization and well-educated medical staff, so we want to achieve the desired condition from 1 January 2025. Finally, a primary healthcare professional will be a specialist in family medicine, in the field of family medicine, in the field of general medicine, in the field of general medicine, in the field of specialist in first or second degree, or in the field of pediatrics. The pediatrician, as so far, will take care of children and young people until they reach the age of 18, subject to completion of the course in the field of family medicine. The funding of POZ will be, as so far, the measures included in the National Health Fund's financial plan. The financial plan for the current year 2017 shows that 9900 million PLN is planned for basic health care, which represents 13.5% planned expenditure on benefits. We are assuming an annual increase in expenditure on the functioning of this part of health care. The new funding system is intended to be a incentive scheme, including: the effectiveness of the treatment, which will ensure the optimal use of the resources allocated to health services. The objectives of the reform will be gradually introduced and further steps will therefore be introduced in the following years when all participants in the system are properly prepared for this. From 2018, we will launch a pilot programme, coordinated care and a new funding system will start from 2020, and family medicine will become a pillar of primary health care from 2025. I ask you to support the bill that is being drafted. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u384	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Basic Healthcare Act is a legal act that is actually expected, indeed is desirable, indeed a difficult act, also technically difficult. For this reason, we have adopted such a concept to develop a kind of way of entering this desired, expected POZ over the coming years. The bill is now needed, but we need to anticipate and anticipate in this bill the current situation with regard to staff, related to habits, related to opportunities, including financial opportunities. Most of the elements discussed here in the form of critical comments are not included in this law. I will try to present collectively the ones that just aroused the most emotions. I will start with matters relating to the transmission of data, because indeed there was a debate at each meeting of the subcommittee and, in principle, every committee meeting that took place. I also hear from you here that there are doubts that these are pharmaceutical companies, and these are private insurers or any other insurers who will reach out to sensitive data about our patients. It was neither the intention of this article nor those records would cause this to be possible, as the provision of data is governed by the Act on the Rights of the Patient and the Ombudsperson of the Rights of the Patient, which sets out the rules for the provision of medical records and the closed catalogue of entities to which the medical records of the patient may be made available. And these two elements in this law are referred to to in order to precisely specify it, to clarify it. The Act on the Rights of the Patient and the ombudsman also recommends that health data be made available to insurance companies. I will recall this because it was not possible, in the response procedure in the subcommittee, that in these laws, firstly, the health care provider makes medical records available to insurance companies with the consent of the patient. It's about art. Article 26 (2) (b) 3 point 7 of the Act on the Rights of the Patient and the Advocate of the Rights of the Patient. Secondly, the application of an insurance undertaking, including a private insurer or any other institution, of information about the health status of the insured person - the scope of the information is determined by the law - requires the written consent of the insured person or of the person to whom the insurance contract is to be concluded, or of his or her statutory representative. And that's an art. Article 38 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 6 Act on Insurance and Insurance Activities. This is the answer for Members who asked for their own data, how we secure them. With regard to the concept of representatives of organisations, health institutions in the area of primary health care, in regulations on the scope of tasks of doctor of the POZ, nurse of the POZ, midwife of the POZ, both from 2005 and 2016, these institutions include among others. Family aid centres, individuals responsible for anti-drugs, family assistance, mental health, patient organisations. The information provided will not contain personal and sensitive data. These are the areas between art. Article 18a 21 specifically recalling those provisions of the Act which prevent the use of sensitive data for which you were properly concerned at a committee meeting. On the other hand, several of you asked about individual questions about funding. I'll answer collectively. We do not have in the Acts on the provision of the indicated funding under any circumstances. Therefore, the POS Act also refers to financing and sources of funding. On the other hand, the novelty of this law concerns additional three elements that regulate the incentive system for doctors who will want to benefit from it. The first element is the budget entrusted. This is in response to questions, where are the resources for diagnostics, extended diagnostics, extended specialist consultations in relation to the current system. The budget entrusted, which used to function shortly before the cashiers of the sick and in three voivodships within the framework of the sickness funds as so-called. fundholding, it was these additional funds to finance the scope of diagnostic benefits, specialist benefits. We want to restore these solutions in a way that is possible right now, now. This will be a project widely extended for 2 years, while at this stage piloting will be carried out by the NFZ on precisely these solutions, i.e. financing additional benefits for an additional budget. Comments on imposing specific extended scopes without funding are also not allowed, because we want to recall this precisely within this budget. And here, too, the answer to those countries that refer to diagnostics. This is an element where these increased diagnostic needs will be financed. It is difficult to enter whether it is not possible to enter an additional budget at this level of care or specifically a percentage share of this budget, because so-called. the list of patients in each physician varies and the diagnostic needs are different for this population. In funding we also point to the possibility of so-called. the task-to-work fee. This includes additional measures to ensure prevention of health promotion within a wide range of health prevention and promotion programmes, which we intend to introduce beyond those already being carried out by the NFZ, so that these activities can be financed precisely, for example. with the participation of nurses, family and environmental nurses, in the patient's environment. We also want to prepare such budgets. Additional action for extra money. The measure of efficiency, it was also one of the questions asked with concern, refers at least to elements that do not exist at the moment. They are in the form of financing specific activities without measuring the therapeutic effect of these activities. This is what we have in preventive programmes funded only if a particular person, a particular potential patient, is involved, and there are no stimulants to reach out to these patients. It is in this task budget that we want to change. And the third element is the motivational supplement for the outcome of treatment and the quality of care provided. This quality has also been mentioned many times here and one of the questions referred to specific quality actions. That question was still valid this year. By drawing up the provisions of this law and indicating the quality of the recruitment process, there has been a strong acceleration in the practice of primary healthcare professionals, in the framework of the accreditation procedure carried out by the Quality Monitoring Centre. According to this project, the first phase of the first accreditation pool will be completed in December. I can't tell you how many practices this will be, because the whole programme affects several hundred actors, and at the first stage it is likely that a few dozen of these practices will be visited. Whether they'll be ready to get such an accreditation certificate, I can't say. I suspect so, because there is a great deal of involvement of doctors working in these practices. On the other hand, the motivational supplement for the outcome of treatment refers to a specific outcome. Here the list, so to speak, such elements can be long depending on the range that will be developed. At the moment, we do not have any studies on these specific programmes or to improve internal quality, because it also sounded here, or as if these elements with specific therapy in a specific case of specific diseases. However, as for the present state, we have experience. The NFZ operates in two categories such programs, which even the therapeutic effect we want to measure. This applies to patients with diabetes mellitus and patients with cardiovascular disease, when prevention programmes or tertiary prevention programmes should be carried out and paid as much as possible for a specific result, which should be indicated, so to speak, for reimbursement purposes. ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u390	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! The draft power market law amends the current electricity market model. In short, this change involves transforming the single-good electricity market into a dual-good market. This means, in practice, that electricity generators, i.e. power plants and power plants, are now being remunerated for the electricity produced and sold, and that, once the power market has been put in place, the generators who enter into a power contract will receive a remuneration not only for the electricity produced and sold, but also for maintaining their generation fleet in full availability. Why is such a change needed? The analysis shows that after 2020 there may be serious generation shortages in Poland in the national electricity system - generation capacity, which is fully controlled and managed by transmission system operators - despite the fact that seven large power blocks are currently being built in the country: two blocks in Opole, one in Jaworzno, Kozienice, Turów, together with those already in Płock and Stalowa Wola, which will be able to cover around 20% peak power demand. What are the reasons why we expect generation shortages in the future? Well, we have a very advanced age of many blocks in our country. They were mainly built in the 1960s, 70s and 80s, especially the 200 MW blocks, which are the basis for production today and which in the country are more than 50. Another reason is the tightening standards for emissions of harmful substances into the environment. This year, specifically in August, the requirements for new best available techniques for electricity and heating were published (so-called. BAT conclusions) which force national manufacturers to adapt to them exhaust after-treatment installations in power plants and CHP plants. This means that significant investment is needed or that generation capacity is excluded which will not be met. new standards. The electricity market is currently very distorted. There are no signals on it to invest in new generation capacity or, for example, in modernization. The market distortion is due to the functioning of RES sources receiving very large subsidies. Working RES sources significantly reduce the working time of controllable sources, i.e. currently conventional sources, thereby aggravating the economic conditions of their operation, while making it necessary to keep these sources ready in the event of a lack of energy production from RES. Here, I would like to strongly defy the kind of suggestions that appear that the power market is in order to fight more strongly renewable energy sources. No, we do not want to combat renewable energy sources, because the agreements we have agreed with the European Commission are that we will implement the commitments imposed on Poland and the commitments made by Poland regarding the amount of renewable energy sources and the reduction of emissions associated with this. Therefore, the power market does not serve this purpose. The power market is designed to ensure that when renewable energy sources are not working, so when less renewable energy is supplied... Even this year, we registered a total of several dozen days when renewable energy sources, whose installed capacity is almost 6 GW, gave up to below 100 MW. Thus, with installed capacity of almost 6 000 renewable energy sources. MW was a few dozen days when the power supplied was less than 100 MW. In such a situation, conventional sources must be ready, they must wait for us to always have a constant supply of electricity. Due to the steady increase in the number of renewable energy sources, the number of hours of operation of conventional energy sources in the country is falling, therefore their economic conditions and the basis for building new energy sources... It is very important that there are worse economic bases for building new conventional energy sources. And it doesn't matter that conventional energy sources are carbon or gas sources. At present, all these conventional energy sources have no basis for pursuing commitments. If we exceed the expected by the European Commission for 2020 15% the share of renewable energy sources, in fact there will no longer be any economic basis for the construction of any conventional power plant. Therefore, in order to maintain the security of the Polish energy system a power market is necessary, it is necessary to conclude such contracts. One thing has nothing to do with the other if we look at whether it's a pro-renewable policy or not. I would also like to explain what has been happening so far. As for all such activities which were, they are treated from the outside as a form of attitude against renewable energy sources, they simply resulted from a completely different approach, connected with the fact that a very large number of Polish population for various reasons did not wish in their immediate vicinity windmill energy sources - and for health reasons, and for a number of other reasons connected precisely with the functioning of local communities. On the other hand, renewable energy sources will be built. The Renewable Energy Act clearly indicates this direction. Other formulas have been adopted for clusters or prosumers, as well as renewable energy for biogas plants. There will be auctions in this direction. So these renewable energy sources will be built - so that we can make this clear. As a government of Poland, we want to continue our work towards fulfilling our commitments with regard to the European Commission. If we look at the next risks, the CO2 emissions trading system is also a threat and the interventions planned in this market are to increase the prices of emission allowances, thereby increasing the cost of electricity generation and further aggravating the economic conditions of power plants and fossil fuel power plants. At the same time, we are in such a situation that in the country there is a growing demand for electricity. According to information in 2016, electricity consumption increased by 2.4% compared to 2015. First, after three quarters, the information indicates that this increase in the current year compared to 2016 will be similar. So it should be assumed that the increase in power demand can be approx. 2% per year. It is also a signal to us that new powers must be built and must be in this direction, so to speak, prepared and that this law is simply essential if we want to maintain the energy security of the country. Continuing in the current model of the electricity market, taking into account the challenges facing our manufacturing sector, would lead to a very serious increase in the problem of capacity adequacy in Poland. As a result, this could lead to irreversible negative consequences for the Polish economy and for the citizens. Such negative effects may be cyclically repeated periods with introduced power limitations, i.e. so-called. power levels. These restrictions have recently taken place in August 2015. In addition to the measurable losses caused by the lack of energy, this would also be a major image loss in Poland. In the event of a small temporary shortage of power in the national energy system, at least part of these shortages may be covered by energy imports from neighbouring electricity systems. However, we cannot allow national consumers to be dependent on permanent imports of electricity, which is not in the interests of our country in the longer term. What does that tell you? It is mainly due to the fact that there is no certainty about the availability of energy from abroad for Polish citizens during periods of danger. Each country in such a situation will first seek to ensure sufficient power for its own citizens. Therefore, the import of capacity should be considered as a complement rather than a basis for State security. For these reasons, it is necessary to rebuild the national manufacturing assets by modernising it and building completely new production blocks in place of decommissioning blocks. The main element of the proposed power market will be the two-stage capacity auctions organised by the transmission system operator. The first auction, called the main auction, will take place 5 years before the year of delivery. We assume that this first auction will take place in the second half of 2018. She's gonna be the main auction. At this auction, the majority of the capacity needed to cover the capacity demand in a given year will be purchased. Then a year before the year of delivery will be organized so-called. an additional auction that will allow for the purchase of any missing power volumes that were not foreseen four years earlier as needed. Power providers who win the auction will receive remuneration just for maintaining their readiness to supply power and for providing it during periods of danger. The risk periods will be announced by the transmission system operator at the time of the national power balance problem. In the event of failure to deliver power, the power supplier shall be liable for failure to comply with its power obligation. This will simply result from a contract with these power suppliers. Capacity providers in the power market will be power plants, heat plants, power stores, electricity consumers, i.e. those who will contractually stop energy collection at a given point in time, i.e. the so-called system. DSRs, and energy sources, RES, which will not use their dedicated support system. As of 2021, a new fee, known as the Capacity Charge, will be set out in the customer account of the existing charges. However, I would like to explain immediately that separating this charge from the current account will not result in an increase in the burden on the recipients. Today, by paying the electricity bill, we bear the cost of maintaining the generation capacity, but it is hidden in this account in the price of energy. This lack of transparency means that ultimately money does not go where it should be, i.e. investment and maintenance of power in energy, but disappears in the overall account of profits and losses of energy companies. We would like the resources collected from the power charge to be where they should be, namely modernisation and new investments. The monthly value of the capacity charge, which will enter into force in 2018 on the basis of the assumption that it will be implemented at the auction in 2021, will vary depending on how much the farms consume energy. So it should be assumed that 3 million of the poorest farms, i.e. using less than 500 kWh of energy per year, will pay 2 PLN per month, even less than 2 PLN per month, i.e. about 20 PLN per year, while in the case of about 1.5 million of these the richest electricity consumers who consume more than 2800 kWh of electricity per year, this monthly fee may amount to about PLN 9,9.5. Looking at the future of our electricity system, but also at the procedure for our relations with the European Commission, I would like to stress that we are at the negotiating stage of the power market and all these things have recently been agreed with the European Commission in connection with the translation of our law over time. As we know, there has been a delay in our work on this bill in the Seym about a month. It was about establishing certain detailed provisions relating to the power market, which will be brought into this law also in the form of amendments agreed with the European Commission to warn questions, because I met with such questions, why we were a little delayed with this process, extending it in time. We would like this bill, when it comes out of the Sejm and goes to the Senate, to be fully in line with the principles that have been developed - I might say so - with the European Commission. On the other hand, as this is all a bit of a package deal, negotiations are also ongoing in parallel with the notification of the second stage of State aid to hard coal mining. As we know, the state aid currently granted ends in 2018, which is less than a year and a half from the end of 2018. If we refer to this, we have this kind of situation that in 2018 there is an end to public aid for coal mining related to the withdrawal of units, i.e. coal mines... [[Lockdown.]] ...in turn, this does not mean that either the Company for the Restructuring of Mines and that these mining areas will not be reorganized. They should even be revitalized. And for this reason, as it will be after 2018, we are currently having very intense discussions on measures and what we want to achieve after 2018 in the mining areas. A special revitalization fund is also being discussed in the field of mining. So that even though the winter package is not fully accepted, in all this, if we are talking about the power market and about restructuring of coal mining, there are also some declarations expected from the Polish government, as these things will continue to look, so what are our expectations regarding the winter package, as we with our energy potential in all of this want to find ourselves. This is still an open discussion, but it is already taking place within the European Commission. We don't want to go out here with these things at the last minute. At the same time, as you know, there are also final discussions on the ETS, i.e. everything related to support, how much money for certificates of origin will be received by individual power plants or how much they will have to pay. We have here a large open package, which also depends on the future of our energy, and actually our economy. This is why we attach great importance to this in the Ministry of Energy and we also want to discuss it quite openly. ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u392	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the Government's draft power market law from print no. 1722. The aim of the regulation under consideration is to ensure a stable and continuous supply of electricity to households and industry. Businesses operating in our country should receive as much power from the electricity grid as they need at the moment and energy supply should be undisturbed and continuous. Unfortunately, we have already had some difficult experiences in Poland. This year Poland was hit by two natural disasters, which each time cut off hundreds of thousands of households from electricity. In the first half of August, nearly 400 thousand. recipients, and in October almost 1400 thousand. consumers have been deprived of access to electricity. In order for the Polish State to be able to fulfil its primary task of ensuring energy security, a stable electricity system is needed, consisting of both a properly developed network of connections, transmission and distribution lines as well as an adequate amount of generation capacity. The fundamental task of such a electricity system is to continuously balance the needs of all customers in the country with the production of electricity in power plants for a total of 365 days a year. As the Minister of Energy has recently mentioned in his speech, the current rules on the energy market must undergo a decisive adjustment. It cannot be that when the wind blows and the sun shines, production takes over RES sources, and in addition they receive subsidies for this production, thus pushing out conventional sources. On the other hand, when the wind stops blowing and the sun does not shine, the whole system saves conventional sources that had to stand by before, which resulted in high maintenance costs without any pay. [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] The power market is about to provide an appropriate premium, in quotes a bonus, for remaining fully available and ready to produce at a time when RES sources are unable to produce energy. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice welcomes the fact that the problem of balancing power in the national electricity system after 2020 was diagnosed by the government in good time and that today the Ministry of Energy came out ready to proceed with the draft law by the Sejm. According to the forecasts of the Polish Power Grids of May 2016 near 2020 there is a possibility of a shortage of capacity reserves within the framework of national generation resources. In such a situation, there may be repeated problems that occurred in August 2015 when the 20th level of power was announced in Poland, which means restrictions on the supply and collection of electricity for industrial customers. According to PSE, without the investment required, the shortfalls after 2020 will increase. In the coming years, Poland will be forced to exclude further developed and less efficient power plants also in connection with the European Union's climate policy: the ETS emissions trading system, the restrictive requirements for emission limit values - BAT conclusions, the Minister mentioned this too, and the risks arising from the winter package proposal. In order to recreate the capacity that will be lacking in the national electricity system, new power plants should be built to ensure the supply of energy, whose level of use is increasing with the economic and industrial development of Poland. The current shape of the energy market based on a single commodity market model does not provide sufficient investment resources and impulses in the manufacturing sector in Poland. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Finally, I would like to say that, in the opinion of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, the draft power market law is filling the current electricity gap, which is a worse treatment of conventional power plants, namely those power plants which, in fact, provide us all with continuous stable access to electricity and without which the correct operation of the entire electricity system would be threatened. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice welcomes the proposed draft law on the power market and is in favour of referring the bill to further work in committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u400	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft power market law. We are in favour of an evolutionary transformation of the Polish electricity system, which we believe should move towards a distributed system, i.e. a system that is environmentally friendly and ensures greater security of electricity supply. The Capacity Market Act may be an important instrument to strengthen the desired direction of change, but it must contain the necessary instruments that will allow the process of creating a decentralised system to be liberated. Therefore, in our view, it is necessary to introduce changes in the draft law which will prevent market concentration and monopolization. In order for the entry into force of the Capacity Market Act to result in the development of a distributed energy system, there should be a reduction of the upper power limit of the production units benefiting from support to a level, say, for example. 50 MW. This will enable the launch of a causal chain that will lead the Polish industry on the Internet of Things and towards efficient power systems managed by artificial intelligence. This will allow the Polish industry to gain a lasting competitive advantage and prevent the periphery of the Polish industrial area. This will be a real completion of the strategy for responsible development of the Deputy Prime Minister of Finance and Development of Mateusz Morawiecki, which aims to break the middle development trap and reindustrialise Polish industry. The adoption of such a direction as the main one in the Power Market Act will allow to launch a process of building a competitive cost-effective, safe for security of supply and green civic energy, where every entrepreneur and every prosumer will have an impact on optimization in terms of energy comfort of their environment of doing business, residence and work. In addition, in this case, independent investors in the energy and industrial sectors can be expected to be active, who will use their financial resources to strengthen the process of reindustrialisation of the country. The law should therefore be considerably simplified and should not contain far-reaching authorisations for implementing bodies because of the need to ensure the stability of the rules over a longer period of time, which is necessary for the formulation of investment incentives. Thus, the Constitution's provision of State security for citizens, including energy security, can be implemented through the formulation of a doctrine of energy security, which will be based on the protection of citizens from large monopolistic organisations. It is also important to bear in mind the EU proposals contained in the winter package, CO2 emissions regulations and BAT recommendations. Dear Minister, The decentralised and distributed generation power market supported by smart real-time markets is in the way of thinking and even ahead of the innovative thinking of Brussels bureaucrats. In addition, small and medium-sized cogeneration installations based on clean coal technologies cooperating with renewable energy sources within the framework of the so-called. hybrid power plants would allow to build modern local thermal energy and generation of electricity, which would also result in the elimination of smog in Poland. If Poles are to rebuild the country's energy potential at 30 GW by charging additional capacity to the power market, it will be a modern distributed energy that meets the requirements of the BAT Directives, including CO2 emission charges and the proposals of the winter package, rather than the one that consolidates inefficient monopolistic structures that hinder the implementation of the Responsible Development Strategy. The power market is today needed by the Polish economy, but it is already necessary to say loudly and decisively that it is only a stage, even a short stage in the pursuit of a modern energy system understood as a electricity market settled on real-time tariffs. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarni Circle, I request that the bill be directed to further work in the Committee on Energy and the Treasury. I think that at this stage this project needs to be further refined, and in the Energy and Treasury Committee we will try to ensure that it is optimally shaped. Thank you very much.

Mr Biernat asked if there was any hidden payment in today's energy bills for the power plant's available capacity? In Poland, there are already some substitutes for such a power market. The intervention cold reserve and the operational power reserve are in place at the moment. Maybe I can say right now that these are fuses used by Polish Power Grids. Such solutions were introduced several years ago to improve energy security in the functioning of the national energy system. In fact, the programme for active customers, i.e. DSR, is already in place. At present, the total cost of already paid capacity market mechanisms is between 700 and 800 million a year. Mr Kubiak asked what would give the market power to our average citizen. In fact, it gives the citizen the same thing as it gives the entrepreneur. Today, however, energy consumers in households are provided with great comfort, which they are not even aware of. This comfort is that we can turn on the TV, washing machine, refrigerator, laptop, light, etc. in our homes at the same time, etc. and we do not wonder whether the right wind is blowing, whether the sun is shining and whether renewable energy sources produce enough energy for our needs. Clearly, there are indications that there is, however, a problem with the current system. This problem is due to the fact that this energy may begin to run out. For this comfort of functioning and the entrepreneur and the citizen to be able to be preserved, it is precisely the power market that will provide us with the right amount of power that is appropriate to our needs. If we consider... Previously, I talked about power, but there is a concrete calculation of the amount of energy in 2016 compared to 2015. There has been an increase of 2.4% in the amount of energy that is received by citizens and by entrepreneurs. This shows that there is a dynamic development of the country. Meanwhile, these 2 years, i.e. the years 2016 and 2017, indicate that... If we consider that 5% this is 1/20 of our energy balance, we can realise that just the entire power plant in Poland is facing a huge civilization challenge, because our electricity consumers, also our home, started to benefit more from this energy. This is also the source that gives us the opportunity to invest in the future when it comes to building new energy blocks, entering the power market and ensuring this stability. Such economic symptoms, which are, give the possibility to obtain much cheaper credits in terms of building the entire manufacturing system in Poland... Even looking at the power market, also considering that money from the power market won't secure the construction costs of all power plants... After all, we are supposed to secure the current costs of their operation. Securing the current costs of their operation and repayment of the loan - that's what the power market is for, while investments will have to be financed by current funds. Only external financing, if there is a power market and there is a guarantee to pay for the period of non-resourced electricity, because at the moment there is no such guarantee, it ensures security, much safer lending. ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-25-sejm-50-1.u460	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! October 16th this year At the age of 80, Dr. Ryszard Kowalczyk, a man of solidarity, a physicist, a great didactician, retired employee of the Institute of Mathematics, Physics and Chemistry at the University of Opole. In 1972 he was sentenced to 25 years in prison, along with his brother Jerzy, who was sentenced to the death penalty, later converted into life imprisonment, in a case which in the annals of history is known as the case of the Kowalskis. On the subject of the event and the process, many articles, studies and several books were created, and I refer to them interested. I'm just gonna say a few words about this. The Academy of Pedagogical Education in Opole was blown up on the night from 5 to 6 October 1971, on the eve of the prepared Academy on the occasion of the 27th anniversary of the Uprising of the Civic Militia and the security authorities of the People's Poland. The authorities there wanted to mark officials pacifying strikes on the Coast in December 1970, and the ceremony itself was supposed to sanction the crime committed, when to peacefully protesting workers, among others. In Gdansk, Elbląg or Szczecin, militiamen shot, as well as troops, killing dozens of them, while many were badly wounded. In one of his interviews, Ryszard Kowalczyk explained the reasons why the brothers decided to blow up the auditorium. The brothers were more concerned with scaring the communist dignitaries and embarrassing them. An explosion that occurred at hours. 0.40, was caused by the detonation of the TNT by electric wires. No one was injured because Jerzy Kowalczyk - contrary to the subsequent accusations of the Polish People's Republic's Prosecutors - carefully checked whether there were any people in the auditorium at the time of the detonation and near it. We gladly and hopefully accepted the next efforts of the independence community to launch the cassation procedure against Richard Kowalczyk, including the initiative of the Minister of Justice Lech Kaczyński, prepared at the request of, among others. The Polish National Remembrance Association and People Repressed in the Polish People's Republic, which took place in 2001 In 2003, the NZS National Commission, together with a group of young councillors from Opole, took the initiative to give Jerzy and Ryszard Kowalczyk honorary citizenship to the city of Opole. In October 2006 in the newly dedicated.Connector of the Opole University of Technology at ul. The Santa Claus exhibition was opened. The Opole road to independence prepared on the 35th anniversary of the actions of the Kowalczyk brothers, that is, blowing up the auditorium of the then Higher Pedagogical School. The second opportunity to prepare the exhibition was 25 years since the announcement of the martial law in Poland. The selection of the material was made by the members of the University of Opole Technical University, and the approval of the authorities allowed the exhibition to be displayed in such a beautiful place. On the other hand, at the initiative of Anna Valentynowicz and Andrzej Stara in 2010. Ryszard and Jerzy Kowalczy were honored with a plaque on the Wall of Remembrance at the Monument of the Fallen Shipyards in Gdansk. In September 2016, the Council adopted Regulation (EU) Patrick Jak, Secretary of State at the Ministry of Justice, proposed to the Opolskie voivode to honor the heroic actions of the Kowalczyk brothers. Adrian Czubak, Opole Voivode, approved the idea of Minister Patryk Jakiego to change the name of one of the halls and since 5 October last year the former Golden Hall in the Opole Provincial Office bears the name of the brothers Jerzy and Ryszard Kowalczyk. At the KW PZPR execution meeting in 1970 in Gdańsk, Zenon Kliszko and Stanisław Kociołek stated: We are dealing with counter-revolution, and counter-revolution must be fought by force. If 300 workers die, the mutiny will be crushed. On the basis of this decision, Army General Wojciech Jaruzelski ordered the shooting of defenseless workers. Militia and the military have used heavy ammunition. They're dead. 5,000 were thrown into the fight against demonstrators. Militia and 27,000. soldiers. 550 tanks and 700 armored transporters were also used. A year later, some of them were about to be decorated in Opole. This is how the Kowalczyks wanted to protest against the reward of murderers. On August 28, 1972, the trial of both brothers began in the Opole Voivodeship Court. The prosecutors demanded the death penalty for both brothers. On September 8, 1972, the Provincial Court in Opole found the Kowalczyk responsible for the explosion in the auditorium. George was sentenced to death, and Richard was sentenced to 25 years in prison. After the protests, the punishment was alleviated for both brothers. Present at the funeral of Sławomir Cenckiewicz, director of the Central Military Archive, emphasized that the people's power did everything to distort the meaning of this act.
